## 2. Technical Deep-Dive: Architecture & Mathematical Foundations

### 1. Computer Vision: Sum of Absolute Differences (SAD) Template Matching
TraceMate Pro features an optical tracking engine designed to keep the reference artwork pinned to the physical paper even if the phone or desk shifts slightly:
1. **Anchor Selection**: When the artist taps a distinct pencil mark on the paper, the system captures a \(32 \times 32\) pixel template patch from a downsampled \(300\text{px}\) compute canvas.
2. **Search Window Scanning**: On every video frame (`requestAnimationFrame`), a \((32 + 2 \times \text{win}) \times (32 + 2 \times \text{win})\) region (\(\text{win} = 20\text{px}\)) is extracted around the last known anchor position.
3. **Luminance Calculation**: RGB pixel channels are converted to perceived luminance using the ITU-R BT.601 standard:
   $$Y = 0.299 \cdot R + 0.587 \cdot G + 0.114 \cdot B$$
4. **Optimized Disparity Minimization**:
   $$\text{SAD}(x, y) = \sum_{py=0, 4, 8\dots}^{31} \sum_{px=0, 4, 8\dots}^{31} |Y_{\text{search}}(x + px, y + py) - Y_{\text{template}}(px, py)|$$
   - By incrementing the search window with step 2 and subsampling pixels with step 4, the inner loop executes only 64 pixel comparisons per candidate window instead of 1,024, enabling execution in **<4ms** on mobile CPUs.
5. **Delta Projection**: If the minimum disparity is below a confidence threshold, the detected displacement \((\Delta x, \Delta y)\) is scaled back to the viewport coordinate system:
   $$\Delta x_{\text{screen}} = \Delta x_{\text{canvas}} \times \left(\frac{\text{Width}_{\text{viewport}}}{\text{Width}_{\text{canvas}}}\right)$$
   and applied to the overlay's CSS translate matrix.

### 2. Gesture Mathematics: Multi-Touch Transformation Engine
TraceMate Pro implements a unified gesture handler on a transparent intercept layer:
- **Euclidean Pinch Distance**:
  $$d = \sqrt{(x_1 - x_0)^2 + (y_1 - y_0)^2}$$
  $$\text{Scale}_{\text{current}} = \text{Scale}_{\text{initial}} \times \left(\frac{d_{\text{current}}}{d_{\text{initial}}}\right)$$
- **Angular Arc Rotation**:
  $$\theta = \text{atan2}(y_1 - y_0, x_1 - x_0) \times \frac{180^\circ}{\pi}$$
  $$\text{Rotation}_{\text{current}} = \text{Rotation}_{\text{initial}} + (\theta_{\text{current}} - \theta_{\text{initial}})$$
- **Hardware-Accelerated Composite Transform**:
  ```css
  transform: translate(imgX px, imgY px) rotate(imgRotate deg) scale(±imgScale, imgScale);
  ```

### 3. Canvas Compositing & Video Recording Pipeline
- Rather than recording the raw camera stream or overlay alone, TraceMate Pro routes video frames into an offscreen recording canvas (`#rec-canvas`).
- A 30 FPS MediaStream is extracted via `recCanvas.captureStream(30)`.
- The stream is piped into a `MediaRecorder` instance initialized with `video/webm;codecs=vp9`.
- When stopped, the recorded memory chunks are converted into an in-memory `Blob` and triggered as a direct client-side `.webm` file download.

- **Implemented a real-time computer vision template matching algorithm** (Sum of Absolute Differences) with 2px stride and 4x pixel subsampling to stabilize paper drift at 60 FPS with <4ms compute latency.
- **Constructed a multi-touch gesture engine** tracking simultaneous 2-finger Euclidean distance pinch-to-zoom, arctangent rotational deltas, and single-finger translation with GPU-accelerated CSS matrix transforms.
- **Integrated mobile hardware APIs**, including `MediaStreamTrack.applyConstraints` for flashlight/torch control, `MediaRecorder` canvas compositing for drawing time-lapses, and Service Worker cache-first offline storage.
- **Designed specialized digital artist utilities**, such as CSS `multiply` blend mode for transparent line art projection, automated interval blinkers for pencil alignment verification, and a 1-pixel micro-stepping nudge pad.

---

## 4. Key Interview Questions & Answers

### Q1: How did you implement real-time computer vision tracking in a browser without causing thermal throttling or frame drops?
> **Answer:** "Processing high-resolution video frames (e.g., 720p or 1080p) via `getImageData` at 60 FPS on mobile devices is computationally expensive because reading pixel data from the GPU back to the CPU stalls the pipeline. To solve this, I designed a multi-tier optimization strategy: First, I downsample the active camera frame onto a small 300px offscreen compute canvas. Second, I isolate a 32x32 pixel template patch representing the user's paper mark. Third, in the Sum of Absolute Differences (SAD) loop, I search within a 20px window using a step size of 2 and subsample only every 4th pixel for luminance calculation using the ITU-R BT.601 formula. This reduced the inner loop comparison operations from over 16,000 down to roughly 400 per frame, bringing the execution time down to under 4ms per frame and maintaining a smooth 60 FPS on mobile devices."

### Q2: How did you handle coordinate space conversions between screen touches, video element rendering, and canvas pixels?
> **Answer:** "Mobile video elements typically use `object-fit: cover` or dynamic aspect ratios that differ from the device viewport and the internal pixel buffer of the canvas. When a user taps the screen to anchor a feature, the event provides screen coordinates (`clientX`, `clientY`). I calculate the bounding client rectangle of the video element, determine the relative coordinate offset within the visible video, and compute a dynamic scaling factor based on the ratio between the compute canvas dimensions and the bounding box (`scaleX = canvas.width / rect.width`). When the tracking algorithm detects a motion vector \((\Delta x, \Delta y)\) on the compute canvas, I invert and multiply that delta by the reciprocal scale factor to shift the overlay image in screen pixels, ensuring pixel-perfect spatial alignment."

### Q3: Why did you build this as a client-side PWA rather than a native iOS/Android application?
> **Answer:** "A primary barrier for artists wanting to use digital tracing tools is friction—downloading large native apps, granting account permissions, dealing with in-app paywalls, and risking cloud storage of their private sketches. By building TraceMate Pro as a client-side PWA using standard W3C Web APIs (`getUserMedia`, Canvas, Service Workers, `MediaRecorder`), users get instant, zero-install access simply by opening a URL. It functions 100% offline via Service Worker caching, stores all data locally in `LocalStorage`, processes all camera frames on-device for total privacy, and can still be installed to the home screen as a standalone full-screen app."

---

## 2. Technical Deep-Dive: Core Engineering & Systems

### 1. Spatial Drag-and-Resize Grid Engine
Unlike traditional dashboard tools that rely on third-party libraries (e.g., Gridster or GridStack), Vyuham implements a native, zero-dependency `GridEngine` class:
- **Matrix Coordinate Extraction**: During `mousedown` on a widget drag handle, the current translation is read directly from computed CSS:
  ```javascript
  const matrix = new DOMMatrix(window.getComputedStyle(el).transform);
  const initialX = matrix.m41;
  const initialY = matrix.m42;
  ```
- **Quantization Math (Grid Snapping)**:
  Position updates during `mousemove` are quantized to a 10px boundary:
  $$\text{newX} = \max\left(0, \text{round}\left(\frac{\text{initialX} + e.\text{clientX} - \text{startX}}{\text{gridSize}}\right) \times \text{gridSize}\right)$$
  $$\text{newY} = \max\left(0, \text{round}\left(\frac{\text{initialY} + e.\text{clientY} - \text{startY}}{\text{gridSize}}\right) \times \text{gridSize}\right)$$
- **Zero-Reflow GPU Acceleration**:
  By applying positions via `el.style.transform = translate(newX px, newY px)`, the browser delegates rendering to the GPU compositor layer, preventing expensive layout thrashing.

### 2. Tab-Throttling Resilient Timekeeping
Modern Chromium browsers throttle timers in inactive tabs to once per minute to conserve battery. A naive timer (`remaining--` every 1000ms) falls behind rapidly:
- **Epoch Target Calculation**:
  When a timer or Pomodoro cycle begins, Vyuham computes an absolute completion epoch:
  $$\text{endTime} = \text{Date.now()} + \text{durationInMilliseconds}$$
- **Tick Delta Evaluation**:
  On every execution tick, the remaining time is calculated as:
  $$\text{remainingSeconds} = \max\left(0, \text{ceil}\left(\frac{\text{endTime} - \text{Date.now()}}{1000}\right)\right)$$
- If the user switches tabs and returns 20 minutes later, the display instantly recalculates the exact remaining time or fires the completion event with mathematical accuracy.

### 3. Recursive Bookmark Tree Rendering
The Bookmarks widget parses Chrome's bookmark storage into a collapsible folder hierarchy:
- Employs a recursive descent function `render(node, parentElement)`.
- If `node.children` exists, it instantiates a collapsible folder element (`.bm-folder`) and recurses for each child node.
- For leaf bookmark nodes, it queries the Chrome internal favicon service:
  ```javascript
  `chrome-extension://${chrome.runtime.id}/_favicon/?pageUrl=${encodeURIComponent(url)}&size=32`
  ```

---

## 3. Resume-Ready Bullet Points

- **Architected a Manifest V3 Chrome Extension new-tab productivity dashboard** utilizing Vanilla JavaScript, HTML5, and CSS3, achieving sub-100ms cold-start load times with zero third-party framework overhead.
- **Engineered a custom 60 FPS drag-and-resize grid system** leveraging `DOMMatrix` affine transformations and 10px mathematical quantization to deliver smooth, hardware-accelerated spatial layout customization.
- **Developed a drift-resilient time tracking suite** (Clock, Countdown Timer, Stopwatch, and Pomodoro) utilizing epoch timestamp deltas (`endTime - Date.now()`) to eliminate timer skew caused by Chromium background tab throttling.
- **Integrated 12 modular widgets**, including an HTML5 drag-and-drop Kanban board, recursive Chrome bookmarks tree traversal, live GitHub contribution heatmaps (`ghchart`), and dynamic AI art wallpapers via the Pollinations API.
- **Implemented client-side data persistence and layout configuration management** utilizing `chrome.storage.local`, featuring debounced state synchronization and JSON-based layout backup/restore capabilities.

---

## 4. Key Interview Questions & Answers

### Q1: How does Manifest V3 differ from Manifest V2, and what architectural constraints did it introduce for your extension?
> **Answer:** "Manifest V3 is Google's major modernization of the Chrome Extension platform, designed to improve security, privacy, and performance. The two biggest architectural differences impacting Vyuham were Content Security Policy (CSP) restrictions and background execution models. MV3 strictly bans remotely hosted code and arbitrary code evaluation (`eval()`), which meant all widget logic, sound assets (`alarm.mp3`), and dependencies had to be bundled locally within the extension package. Additionally, external resources such as site favicons must now go through Chrome's dedicated favicon service (`chrome-extension://.../_favicon/`) rather than ad-hoc Google scrapers, which required declaring explicit `favicon` permissions in `manifest.json`."

### Q2: How did you solve the problem of timer inaccuracy when Chrome background tabs are throttled?
> **Answer:** "Chromium aggressively throttles `setInterval` and `setTimeout` down to once per minute or suspends them altogether when a tab is inactive or minimized. If you decrement a counter by 1 every second, your timer will pause or lose minutes while the user is working in another tab. To solve this, I designed the timer and Pomodoro systems to be stateful based on real-time epoch timestamps. When the timer starts, I calculate `endTime = Date.now() + duration`. On each tick—or whenever the user re-focuses the tab—the remaining duration is computed dynamically as `Math.ceil((endTime - Date.now()) / 1000)`. Even if the browser throttled the timer loop, the calculation immediately recovers the true elapsed time without any drift."

### Q3: Why did you build your own grid drag-and-resize engine instead of using a library like GridStack or React Grid Layout?
> **Answer:** "In a Chrome extension that overrides the new tab page, user experience depends heavily on instantaneous startup speed. Bundling a heavy framework like React along with layout libraries adds significant bundle weight, script parsing overhead, and DOM hydration delay. By writing a lightweight native JavaScript `GridEngine` class using `DOMMatrix` coordinate extraction and CSS `transform: translate(x, y)`, the extension avoids layout thrashing, achieves hardware-accelerated 60 FPS dragging, has zero external NPM dependencies, and opens in under 100ms."

## 2. Technical Deep-Dive

### 1. Inverted Room Extraction Pipeline (`extract_rooms.py`)
College timetables are constructed from the perspective of academic classes (e.g. Third Year Computer Engineering Div A), not physical rooms. To build a door-mounted QR system, the entire data structure must be mathematically inverted:
1. **Header Inspection**: Regex inspects the first 10 rows and sheet titles for primary classroom identifiers (`r"(?:Lect\.?\s*Hall\s*No\.?-|Class\s*room:|Room\s*No\.?\s*[:\.-]?)\s*([A-Za-z0-9\-]+)"`).
2. **Cell-Level Room Extraction**: For practical lab sessions where multiple batches (e.g., B1, B2, B3) work simultaneously, individual cells embed multiple room codes (`"524/526/527"`). The script regex-splits these values (`re.findall(r"\b\d{3}[A-Z]?\b", part)`), fans the slot out to all referenced physical rooms, and appends the appropriate subject string.
3. **Collision Merging**: When multiple batches or lecture divisions share a room, conflicting time slots are merged:
   ```python
   key = (slot["day"], slot["slot"])
   if key not in merged_slots:
       merged_slots[key] = slot
   else:
       merged_slots[key]["subject"] += " <br>---<br> " + slot["subject"]
   ```

### 2. Temporal Slot Matching & Heuristic Parsing (`app.js`)
Timetable slots in the wild contain irregular punctuation (hyphens, en-dashes `–`, periods instead of colons, missing AM/PM tags). The client-side parser normalizes these variations:
- **Heuristic PM Conversion**: Because college lectures between 1:00 and 6:00 PM are frequently transcribed as bare numbers (e.g., `"1:15 to 2:15"`), the parser applies:
  ```javascript
  if (!period && hour >= 1 && hour <= 6) {
      hour += 12; // Afternoon college session
  }
  ```
- **Interval Check**: Converts start and end times to cumulative minutes since midnight, checking against the client device's `Date` object:
  ```javascript
  const currentMin = now.getHours() * 60 + now.getMinutes();
  return currentMin >= startMin && currentMin < endMin;
  ```
- **Break Normalization**: Entries matching `"Free"`, `"Break"`, `"Recess"`, or `"Vacant"` are automatically marked as `Available Now` with green indicator badges.

## 4. Key Interview Questions & Answers

### Q1: How did your Python data pipeline resolve merged cells and conflicting timetable versions across multiple Excel workbooks?
> **Answer:** "College timetables are notoriously difficult to automate because they are formatted for visual printing with merged cells, multiple revisions, and multi-batch entries. In `import_timetable_xls.py`, I built an adapter pattern with `XlrdSheetAdapter` and `OpenpyxlSheetAdapter` that checks `sheet.merged_cells` and automatically propagates parent values to all merged children. To handle conflicting timetable revisions across semesters, I implemented a scoring pass: it parses 'With Effect From' (W.E.F.) dates via regex, scores each sheet based on valid non-empty slot count, and filters out draft or duplicate sheets (such as sheets marked with '(2)'), ensuring only the most recent authoritative schedule is exported to `data.json`."

### Q2: How does the client-side JavaScript engine determine room availability in real time without a backend server?
> **Answer:** "Rather than relying on a database server to query availability, the entire normalized campus schedule (26 rooms) is loaded into client memory via a single lightweight JSON payload (~110KB). In `app.js`, I built a temporal evaluation engine: when a student scans a QR code, the app checks the current day of the week and iterates through the room's timetable slots for that day. A custom `parseTime()` function normalizes messy time strings (handling en-dashes, periods, and campus conventions where hours 1 to 6 without AM/PM tags represent afternoon sessions). It converts both the slot bounds and the current local clock time into minutes since midnight. If current minutes fall within an active slot interval and the subject is not a break or recess, the UI immediately flags the room as 'Occupied - [Subject Name]'; otherwise, it displays an emerald 'Available Now' badge, polling every 60 seconds to update status dynamically."

### Q3: How did you handle multi-batch lab schedules where multiple lab rooms were listed inside a single cell?
> **Answer:** "In engineering colleges, practical lab sessions are scheduled concurrently: a single class will divide into batches B1, B2, and B3, with each batch assigned to a different computer lab (e.g. 'B1: 524, B2: 526, B3: 527'). In `extract_rooms.py`, I implemented an inverted extraction algorithm. The script parses each table cell, executes a regex search (`r'\b\d{3}[A-Z]?\b'`) to isolate all room codes, and fans the slot out to every identified room's schedule array. If two batches or conflicting entries land on the exact same room and time slot, the pipeline detects the composite key `(day, slot)` and cleanly merges them using `<br>---<br>` delimiters, ensuring that students scanning either lab's doorway QR code see the exact batch assignment for that room."

## Technical Deep-Dive & Interview Talking Points

### Top Technical Interview Questions & Answers

#### Q1: "How did you implement dynamic page routing and data hydration without a router library like React Router?"
> **Answer**:  
> I used standard Web Platform APIs, specifically `window.location.search` and `URLSearchParams`. When a user clicks 'Download' or a game card on the home page, the browser navigates to `game-details.html?id=${game.id}`. In `game-details.js`, on `DOMContentLoaded`, `new URLSearchParams(window.location.search).get('id')` extracts the parameter. It then fetches `games.json`, performs an \(O(N)\) search via `data.find(g => g.id === gameId)`, and imperatively populates the DOM nodes with the corresponding game title, system requirements, cover, and screenshot gallery.

#### Q2: "How does your live search filter work, and how does it handle UX prioritization?"
> **Answer**:  
> In `scripts.js`, the search bar listens to the `input` event. As the user types, the query string is normalized with `.toLowerCase()`. An array filter selects all games where `game.title.toLowerCase().includes(query)`. The function `displayGames()` re-renders the filtered games into their matching category columns. To enhance UX, if a query is active, the first matching result (`filteredGames[0]`) is highlighted and mounted directly into the prominent `#featured-games` banner, giving the user immediate visual feedback of their top search match.

#### Q3: "What are the architectural benefits of decoupling your data into `games.json`?"
> **Answer**:  
> Decoupling data into `games.json` implements a headless content architecture. Instead of hardcoding 18 distinct HTML cards across multiple category sections, the HTML remains clean and minimal. Adding, modifying, or removing games (or changing categories) only requires editing the JSON document. Furthermore, `games.json` can be fetched once and cached by the browser, drastically minimizing network overhead and keeping data synchronized between the catalog page and the detail page.

## Technical Deep-Dive & Interview Talking Points

### Top Technical Interview Questions & Answers

#### Q1: "Why did you choose a pure CSS lightbox and mobile menu over JavaScript libraries?"
> **Answer**:  
> For a rural public school website, many parents and visitors access the site on low-cost smartphones over slower 3G/4G cellular networks. By leveraging modern CSS features—such as the `:target` pseudo-class for modal popups and the hidden-checkbox hack for the mobile hamburger navigation—I eliminated third-party JavaScript dependencies. This dramatically reduced the page's Time to Interactive (TTI) and First Input Delay (FID), allowing instant modal interactions and 60 FPS transitions driven entirely by the browser's native compositing engine.

#### Q2: "How did you ensure bilingual readability between Marathi Devanagari script and English typography?"
> **Answer**:  
> The site integrates Marathi Devanagari headings for the school's official title ("जिल्हा परिषद प्राथमिक आदर्श शाळा मुली लासुरगाव") alongside English body copy and labels. Because Devanagari characters have distinct vertical ascenders and descenders compared to Latin fonts, I established custom line-heights (`1.9em`) and clean font fallbacks to prevent font clipping or uneven vertical alignment across differing browser rendering engines.

#### Q3: "How does the CSS `:target` modal popup work behind the scenes?"
> **Answer**:  
> Each gallery thumbnail links to an anchor ID (e.g. `<a href="#gal1">`). The modal overlay div has `id="gal1"` with `opacity: 0` and `visibility: hidden` by default. When clicked, the URL hash becomes `#gal1`, which matches the `:target` pseudo-class in CSS (`.pop-overlay:target`). This rule dynamically flips `opacity: 1` and `visibility: visible` with a smooth transition. Inside the popup, a close button links back to `#gallery`, removing the hash and smoothly closing the modal without a single line of JavaScript.
